How it worksResultsChangesConfusionBreakdownToolsCases

How this eval works

Each case gives the agent one germline variant (HGVS on a RefSeq transcript), its gene and a condition, through the server's interpret_variant MCP prompt, exactly as a user would invoke /mcp__genomics__interpret_variant. The agent can call the server's tools and read its resources, then must call submit_answer with a 5-tier classification and the ACMG/AMP criteria it applied. ClinVar's per-variant classification tool is hidden, because ClinVar is the source of the gold labels.

Gold labels

ClinVar germline classifications with review status reviewed by expert panel or practice guideline (3–4 stars), sampled round-robin across ClinGen VCEP genes, 12 per tier. Expert panels can use functional, segregation and case-level evidence that no public aggregate source provides, so a perfect score is not attainable from these tools. A second, disjoint held-out set (different seed, no shared variants) measures changes that were developed while looking at the main set's failures.

System prompt (host + MCP server instructions)
You are an assistant connected to the genomics MCP server. Follow the user's request. When you have finished, call submit_answer once with your final classification and the criteria you applied.

Public genomics evidence for germline variant interpretation (GRCh38 only).

Variants can be given as VCF-style ids (17-43045712-T-C), rsIDs, or HGVS with a
versioned RefSeq accession (NM_007294.4:c.5266dup). Every tool normalizes its
input; call normalize_variant first only when an rsID may be multi-allelic or
you need the canonical id. Upstream APIs are rate-limited and Ensembl can be
slow (10-60 s); call tools for one variant at a time and reuse results.

Read genomics://acmg-criteria for how each tool's output maps to ACMG/AMP
criteria. State which criteria you apply, at what strength, and which ones
cannot be evaluated from public aggregate data.

For research and education only. Output is not a clinical interpretation.

ClinVar is not available in this session: interpret from primary evidence.
Example user message (from the interpret_variant prompt)
Interpret the germline variant NM_003159.3:c.3031G>A (CDKL5).
Condition of interest: CDKL5 disorder

1. Normalize the variant and confirm the gene and MANE Select transcript.
2. Get the predicted consequence and in-silico scores.
3. Get gnomAD frequency (use grpmax FAF95 for frequency criteria) and gene constraint.
4. Read genomics://acmg-criteria. For each criterion you can evaluate, state the code, strength and the specific value that triggers it. List the criteria that need data not available here.

Finish with a 5-tier classification (Pathogenic, Likely pathogenic, Uncertain significance, Likely benign, Benign), the criteria combination that supports it, and your main uncertainty. This is a research exercise, not a clinical report.

Metrics

P/LP sensitivityOf variants the panel calls Pathogenic or Likely pathogenic, the share the agent puts in P/LP. A miss is a missed actionable finding. Headline.
P↔B flipsPathogenic-side gold called benign-side, or the reverse. The most harmful single error. Headline, lower is better.
B/LB, VUS correctThe same share for benign-side and VUS gold.
3-tier / 5-tierAgreement after collapsing to P/LP, VUS, B/LB; and exact tier agreement.
Self-consistentReference-free: the submitted tier equals the tier implied by the agent's own criteria under the ACMG point system (Supporting 1, Moderate 2, Strong 4, Very strong 8; P ≥10, LP 6–9, VUS 0–5, LB −1 to −6, B ≤−7).
Intervals95% Wilson score intervals. With 24 P/LP cases per set, differences under ~20 points are within noise.

P → VUS CDKL5 NM_001323289.2:c.215T>C missense_variant · 5 tools · 28s

Condition: CDKL5 disorder · Gold: Pathogenic (VCV000011503.25, reviewed by expert panel, evaluated 2021-03-26)

codestrengthevidence the agent cited
PM2supportingNot found in gnomAD v4 (exomes and genomes), so no grpmax FAF95 is available. Applied at Supporting per ClinGen SVI 2020. Site coverage and hemizygous calls were not checked.
PM1moderatep.Ile72Thr is in the CDKL5 catalytic kinase domain (about residues 13-297), close to the ATP-binding region (Lys42). Pathogenic missense variants in CDKL5 cluster in this domain. This rests on domain knowledge, not tool output, and I did not check whether residue 72 is on the ClinGen Rett/Angelman VCEP's list of residues that qualify for PM1.
PP2supportingCDKL5 is depleted of missense variants in gnomAD (missense Z = 4.63, o/e = 0.60) and also has very few loss-of-function variants (pLI = 1.0, LOEUF = 0.23). Missense changes are an established cause of CDKL5 deficiency disorder. The gene-specific VCEP may restrict PP2.
PP3supportingAlphaMissense 0.989 (likely pathogenic), CADD 26.3, PolyPhen 0.995 (probably damaging), SIFT 0.02 (deleterious, low confidence). SpliceAI max delta = 0, so no predicted splicing effect. No REVEL score was returned, so the server's calibrated REVEL thresholds could not be used and PP3 was kept at Supporting.

Not evaluable: PS2 and PM6 (de novo status, which is common in CDKL5 deficiency disorder), PS3 and BS3 (functional or kinase-activity studies), PS4 (case-control data), PP1 and BS4 (segregation), PP4 (patient phenotype), PS1 and PM5 (other pathogenic changes at codon 72; ClinVar not available in this session), BP2 and BP5 (case data). BS2 is only partly evaluable: no hemizygous males were seen because the variant is absent from gnomAD.

Summary: NM_001323289.2:c.215T>C (MANE Select; GRCh38 X-18575423-T-C, rs62641235) is a missense change, p.Ile72Thr, in exon 5 of 18 within the CDKL5 kinase domain. It is absent from gnomAD v4, and in-silico predictors consistently score it as damaging (AlphaMissense 0.989, CADD 26.3, PolyPhen 0.995; no splicing effect predicted). The gene is constrained against both missense and loss-of-function variants. Criteria applied: PM1 (Moderate) + PM2_Supporting + PP2 + PP3 = 5 points, which falls in the Uncertain significance range and leans toward pathogenic. Main uncertainty: the result would become Likely pathogenic if PP3 were raised to Moderate with a calibrated predictor (AlphaMissense about 0.99, or REVEL if available) or if case-level evidence such as de novo status (PS2 or PM6) were available. Whether the VCEP's PM1 and PP2 rules apply to this variant was also not confirmed. Research use only.

Tool calls

  1. normalize_variant(variant=NM_001323289.2:c.215T>C)
  2. read_mcp_resource(uri=genomics://acmg-criteria)
  3. annotate_variant(variant=X-18575423-T-C, gene=CDKL5)
  4. get_population_frequency(variant=X-18575423-T-C)
  5. get_gene_constraint(gene_symbol=CDKL5)

Condition: DICER1-related tumor predisposition · Gold: Pathogenic (VCV000690480.15, reviewed by expert panel, evaluated 2022-05-17)

codestrengthevidence the agent cited
PM1moderatep.Asp1709Asn changes D1709, one of the metal-ion-binding catalytic residues of the DICER1 RNase IIIb domain (exon 24/27). It is a recognised mutational hotspot (E1705, D1709, G1809, D1810, E1813) where missense changes stop 5p miRNA production. This comes from domain knowledge; the tools here only give the protein position.
PM2supportingNot observed in gnomAD v4 (exome + genome). Applied at Supporting per ClinGen SVI 2020. Site coverage was not checked.
PP3moderateREVEL 0.868 is at or above the 0.773 Moderate threshold (Pejaver 2022). Other tools agree but were not counted separately: AlphaMissense 0.9975 (likely pathogenic), CADD 30, SIFT 0, PolyPhen 0.993. SpliceAI max delta 0, so no predicted splice effect.

Not evaluable: PS1 and PM5 need ClinVar or curated lists of other changes at codon 1709 (for example D1709E/G/Y/V), and ClinVar was not available. PS3 needs functional literature, such as in vitro miRNA-processing assays of D1709N, which was not available. PS2, PM6, PP1, PP4, PS4, BS4, BP2 and BP5 need case, family or de novo data. Germline vs mosaic status also cannot be assessed, which matters because RNase IIIb hotspot variants are usually somatic second hits or mosaic. Not applied: PP2, because germline disease is mostly loss-of-function (pLI 1, LOEUF 0.286) and missense constraint is only moderate (missense Z 6.28, o/e 0.69); BP1, because hotspot missense variants are an established mechanism. PVS1, PM4, BA1/BS1/BS2, BP4 and BP7 do not apply.

Summary: NM_177438.3:c.5125G>A normalises to GRCh38 14-95094127-C-T (rs1595331264). It is in DICER1, MANE Select ENST00000343455.8 / NM_177438.3, and causes p.Asp1709Asn, a missense change in exon 24/27 at a catalytic metal-binding residue of the RNase IIIb domain. It is absent from gnomAD v4. REVEL 0.868 (PP3 at Moderate), AlphaMissense 0.9975, CADD 30, and no predicted splice effect. Criteria: PM1 (Moderate) + PM2_Supporting + PP3_Moderate = 5 Bayesian points, one point short of Likely pathogenic (6). Classification from the data available here: Uncertain significance, leaning towards pathogenic. Main uncertainty: this is a well-known somatic hotspot residue. Published functional data (PS3) or established pathogenic variants at codon 1709 (PS1/PM5) would probably raise it to Likely pathogenic or Pathogenic, but neither could be checked without ClinVar or literature. A germline finding should also be checked for mosaicism, because these hotspot variants are typically somatic or mosaic. Research use only.

LP → VUS PTPN11 NM_002834.5:c.782T>A missense_variant · 5 tools · 44s

Condition: RASopathy · Gold: Likely pathogenic (VCV000575203.9, reviewed by expert panel, evaluated 2020-03-16)

codestrengthevidence the agent cited
PM1moderatep.Leu261His sits in the PTP catalytic domain of SHP2, at a PTP-domain residue that contacts the N-SH2 domain at the autoinhibitory interface. That interface is a recognised RASopathy gain-of-function hotspot. Domain knowledge (from memory, not checked here) suggests residue 261 is on the ClinGen RASopathy VCEP PTPN11 PM1 residue list. Applied provisionally at Moderate.
PM2supportinggnomAD v4 joint AC=1/1,612,882 (AF 6.2e-7), single allele in 'Remaining' exome; no grpmax FAF95 computable; exome call fails AS_VQSR. Extremely rare, consistent with a dominant, early-onset, highly penetrant disorder (SVI: Supporting).
PP2supportingPTPN11 is strongly depleted of missense variation (missense Z = 5.61, o/e 0.52, upper 0.56), and RASopathy is caused mainly by gain-of-function missense changes. The RASopathy VCEP allows PP2 for PTPN11.

Not evaluable: PS2/PM6 (de novo status), PS3/BS3 (functional assays of SHP2 phosphatase activity or RAS-MAPK signalling), PS4 (case-control or proband counts), PP1/BS4 (segregation), PP4 (phenotype), PS1/PM5 (ClinVar unavailable, so I could not check other pathogenic alleles at Leu261 such as p.Leu261Phe), BP2/BP5 (case data). PVS1 does not apply: this is a missense change, and RASopathy is a gain-of-function mechanism. PP3/BP4: REVEL 0.558 falls in the uninformative 0.290–0.644 range and below the VCEP PP3 cut-off of 0.7. AlphaMissense 0.587 (likely pathogenic, borderline) and CADD 22.4 lean deleterious, while SIFT (tolerated) and PolyPhen (benign, 0.022) do not, so computational evidence is not applied. SpliceAI = 0, so there is no splice effect. BA1/BS1/BS2: frequency is far below any benign threshold.

Summary: NM_002834.5:c.782T>A normalises to 12-112472969-T-A (GRCh38), rs765642157, in PTPN11 exon 7/16 of the MANE Select transcript (ENST00000351677.7). It is a missense change, p.Leu261His, in the PTP domain. gnomAD v4 has 1 allele out of about 1.6 million, with no FAF95 computable. The gene is highly constrained: pLI 1.0, LOEUF 0.15, missense Z 5.61. In-silico tools disagree: REVEL 0.558 is uninformative, AlphaMissense 0.587 is borderline likely pathogenic, CADD is 22.4, and SIFT and PolyPhen call it benign. SpliceAI is 0. Applied criteria: PM1 (Moderate, provisional), PM2_Supporting and PP2. That totals about 4 Bayesian points, which is Uncertain significance. Main uncertainty: whether Leu261 counts as an established PM1 hotspot residue, and whether pathogenic missense changes at the same codon exist (PM5). Without case data (de novo status, segregation, phenotype) or functional evidence, the variant stays a VUS. If de novo status were confirmed (PS2) or functional data showed gain of function (PS3), it would reach Likely pathogenic. Research use only.

These cysteines form the repeat's disulfide bonds and are a well-established critical site; the ClinGen FH VCEP lists LA-repeat cysteines as PM1-eligible. This relies on domain knowledge, not tool output.

Summary: LDLR NM_000527.5:c.905G>A, p.Cys302Tyr (GRCh38 19-11107479-G-A, rs879254715). It is a missense variant in exon 6 of the MANE Select transcript ENST00000558518.6. Main uncertainty: case-level and functional data are missing, and no ClinVar data was available to check other changes at this codon. PM5 or PS3/PS4/PP1/PP4 evidence, which is likely to exist for a cysteine change in an LDLR LA repeat, would probably move this to Likely pathogenic or Pathogenic. Research use only.

Condition: ATM-related cancer predisposition · Gold: Likely pathogenic (VCV000142187.64, reviewed by expert panel, evaluated 2024-11-26)

codestrengthevidence the agent cited
PM2supportinggnomAD v4 joint AC 18/1,614,004 (AF 1.12e-5), 0 homozygotes, no QC filters; grpmax FAF95 = 6.88e-6 (NFE). That is rare enough for ATM, and is applied at Supporting per SVI 2020.
PP3supportingMissense p.Arg3008Cys. AlphaMissense 0.9486 (likely_pathogenic), CADD 28.1, SIFT 0 (deleterious, low confidence), PolyPhen 0.892. REVEL was not returned, so the calibrated REVEL thresholds could not be used. Kept at Supporting; AlphaMissense calibration might allow Moderate. SpliceAI max 0.02, so no splice effect predicted.

Not evaluable: PS3/BS3 (functional assays of the R3008 residue in the PRD/FATC-adjacent region; published kinase-activation studies exist but are not available from these tools). PM3 (in trans with a pathogenic ATM variant in ataxia-telangiectasia patients; case data). PS4 (case-control enrichment in breast/pancreatic cancer). Summary: NM_000051.4:c.9022C>T maps to GRCh38 11-108365359-C-T (rs587782292). It is in ATM, MANE Select ENST00000675843.1, exon 63/63, and causes the missense change p.Arg3008Cys. The residue is in the C-terminal PIKK regulatory domain, just upstream of FATC. ATM shows no LoF constraint in gnomAD (pLI ~0, LOEUF 0.87), but this is known for ATM, where heterozygous loss of function confers moderate cancer risk. gnomAD v4 has the allele as rare (AC 18, grpmax FAF95 6.88e-6) with no homozygotes, giving PM2_Supporting. In-silico tools agree it is damaging (AlphaMissense 0.949, CADD 28.1), giving PP3_Supporting, and there is no predicted splice effect. PM2_P + PP3 adds up to about 2 points, which is Uncertain significance. Main uncertainty: this codon is reported in the literature in A-T patients, and R3008 substitutions have been studied functionally. If those data were confirmed (PS3 and/or PM3 from compound-heterozygous A-T cases), along with ClinVar evidence at this codon (PS1/PM5), the variant could move to Likely pathogenic. From public aggregate data alone it stays a VUS. This is a research exercise, not a clinical interpretation.

Condition: Glycogen storage disease, type II · Gold: Likely pathogenic (VCV000984800.8, reviewed by expert panel, evaluated 2024-09-17)

codestrengthevidence the agent cited
PP3supportingSynonymous p.Leu680= at the last base of exon 14 (c.2040G>A, ctG>ctA; the next base is c.2040+1, the canonical donor). SpliceAI max delta 0.86 (donor loss 0.86 at the exon 14 donor; donor gain 0.19 at +26), well above the 0.2 cutoff for splicing PP3. CADD 19.3. Applied at Supporting per ClinGen SVI splicing guidance (Walker 2023).
PM2supportinggnomAD v4 joint AC=1/1,608,970 (AF 6.2e-7, one NFE heterozygote, 0 homozygotes). grpmax FAF95 is not calculable because AC=1. Extremely rare, which fits a recessive Pompe allele. Applied at Supporting per SVI 2020.

Not evaluable: PVS1: the variant is exonic, at the last nucleotide, not at canonical ±1/2. Under SVI splicing guidance it needs RNA evidence (exon 14 skipping or a frame effect) before PVS1 can be applied at any strength. PS3/BS3 need RT-PCR, minigene or GAA enzyme activity studies. PM3 needs case data on phase with a pathogenic GAA variant. PP4 needs a patient phenotype or GAA enzyme deficiency (the LSD VCEP uses this for Pompe).
